Jesduvroq (daprodustat)

Conclusion
The NDA data for daprodustat do not identify a significant liver injury risk that would preclude a
favorable benefit-risk assessment. However, there were issues regarding missing data (e.g.,
alkaline phosphatase) that may have impaired a more thorough evaluation. The DILI team
recommends labeling to monitor liver enzymes and bilirubin at baseline and as clinically
indicated and recommends including liver injury in a post market pharmacovigilance strategy.

7.8. Risk and Evaluation Mitigation Strategy
The Division of Risk Management (DRM) and Office of Medication Error Prevention and Risk
Management (OMEPRM) were consulted. The conclusions from their review are summarized
below.
Based on the available data, a risk evaluation and mitigation strategy is not necessary to ensure
the benefits of daprodustat in the DD population outweigh the risks. The safety concerns
associated with daprodustat use are similar to ESAs including the risk of MACE and healthcare
providers who treat anemia of CKD are familiar with and able to appropriately monitor these
risks.
If an Applicant resubmits daprodustat for use in the NDD population, a re-evaluation of the need
for a risk evaluation and mitigation strategy will be conducted at that time.
For additional details, refer to the review by Carla Darling, PharmD, BCPS, Jacqueline
Sheppard, Pharm D and Cynthia LaCivita, Pharm D, dated January 26, 2023 in DARRTs.

8. Therapeutic Individualization
8.1. Intrinsic Factors
Hepatic Impairment
The Applicant did not propose any dose modifications for patients with mild or moderate hepatic
impairment. Patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child Pugh C) were not studied, and the
Applicant recommends that treatment with daprodustat be avoided in that population.
Mild and Moderate Hepatic Impairment
A dedicated hepatic impairment study (Study 200231) was conducted where participants with
mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh Class A (Score 5 to 6)), moderate hepatic impairment
(Child-Pugh Class B (Score 7 to 9)) and their matched healthy controls with normal hepatic
function were administered a single 6 mg oral dose of daprodustat in a fasted state. Results are
shown below in Table 110.
Daprodustat Cmax and AUC geometric least square mean ratios [90% CI] were 1.9-fold [1.06,
3.71] and 2.0-fold [1.10, 3.61] higher, respectively, in subjects with moderate hepatic
impairment (Cohort 1) compared with their matched healthy control subjects (Cohort 2). All
metabolites exposure geometric least square mean ratios were between 1.2-fold and 1.7-fold

Jesduvroq (daprodustat)

higher in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment (Cohort 1) compared with their matched
healthy control subjects (Cohort 2).
Cmax for daprodustat was similar between subjects with mild hepatic impairment (Cohort 3) and
their matched healthy controls (Cohort 4). The Cmax geometric least square mean ratio [90% CI]
was 1.01 [0.71, 1.43]. The daprodustat AUC geometric least square mean ratio [90% CI] was
1.46-fold [1.03, 2.05] higher in subjects with mild hepatic impairment compared with their
matched healthy control subjects. Cmax and AUC geometric least square mean ratios for all
metabolites were between 1.3-fold and 2.0-fold higher in subjects with mild hepatic impairment
compared with their matched healthy control subjects.
Daprodustat is highly protein bound. The fraction unbound (fu) in the 3-hour post-dose plasma
samples was 0.0034 in the participants with moderate hepatic impairment and 0.0028 in their
matched healthy controls. Unbound AUC and Cmax of daprodustat were 2.2-fold to 2.3-fold
higher in subjects with moderate and mild hepatic impairment compared to their matched healthy
cohorts. However, high variability (33.2-578% CV) was noted for the unbound PK parameters.
In general, time to maximum concentration (Tmax, median ~1.5 hours for daprodustat and 3 to 4
hours for the metabolites) and the t½ (mean ~4 to 4.5 h for daprodustat and 2.7 to 5.6 h for the
metabolites) was similar across Cohorts 1 to 4.

Following single oral administration of 6 mg of daprodustat, arithmetic mean peak and systemic
EPO levels (Cmax, EPO and AUC[0-t,EPO]) in subjects with hepatic impairment were between 1.2fold (mild) and 1.8-fold (moderate) those of matched healthy controls. This increase in EPO
observed in hepatically impaired subjects is consistent with the increased daprodustat exposure.

(Semilogarithmic Scale)

Source: Study 200231 CSR Figure 2.
Note: LLOQ = 2.50 IU/L. Dashed line represents LLOQ
Cohort 1 = Moderate hepatic impairment, Cohort 2 = Matched healthy controls for moderate hepatic impairment Cohort 3 = Mild
hepatic impairment, Cohort 4 = Matched healthy controls for mild hepatic impairment

Daprodustat is titrated to attain and maintain a target hemoglobin level of 11 g/dL. It is important
to prevent a rapid increase in Hb or attaining too high a Hb level (>11 g/dL) in patients with
CKD as rapid or excessive Hb increases have been associated with the risk of severe adverse
cardiovascular events and death in patients treated with ESAs, another treatment for anemia of
CKD. A 2-fold increase in exposure of daprodustat in patients with moderate hepatic impairment
may be associated with too large an increase in Hb. Therefore, a reduction of the starting dose of
daprodustat in patients with moderate hepatic impairment is recommended to account for the
increase in the systemic exposure of daprodustat.
We recommend reducing the starting dose of daprodustat by approximately half when initiating
treatment in patients with moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh Class B) except in patients

192
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
[To complete this review, detailed instructions are available in the Integrated Review Template How-to Guide.]
Reference ID: 5119138

NDA 216951
Jesduvroq (daprodustat)

whose starting dose is already 1 mg, the lowest available strength of daprodustat. No adjustment
of the starting dose is required in patients with mild hepatic impairment.
Severe Hepatic Impairment
The Applicant has not evaluated the effect of severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh Class C) on
daprodustat PK. Subjects with severe hepatic impairment and history of chronic liver disease
(e.g., cirrhosis or fibrosis of the liver) were excluded from the phase 3 studies. The unbound
exposure of daprodustat is expected to further increase in patients with severe hepatic
impairment compared to moderate hepatic impairment due to more progressive
hypoalbuminemia. As there is no PK and clinical safety experience for daprodustat in patients
with severe hepatic impairment, use of daprodustat in patients with severe hepatic impairment is
not recommended.
Other Intrinsic Factors
Renal Impairment
Daprodustat PK was evaluated in single dose (Study PHI112843) study in subjects with
moderate to severe renal impairment and matched healthy cohort, and in a multiple dose study
(Study PHI115573) in subjects with NDD renal impairment (Stages 3/4/5 CKD), and subjects
with Stage 5 CKD on hemodialysis. Daprodustat PK following single or repeat doses was similar
between subjects with moderate to severe renal impairment (CKD Stages 3 to 4) and their
matched healthy controls (see Section 14.2 for details). Daprodustat PK was similar between
subjects on hemodialysis on a non-dialysis day and subjects with moderate to severe renal
impairment. Daprodustat is not significantly cleared by hemodialysis. For subjects with Stage 3/4
non-dialysis and Stage 5 CKD on hemodialysis (dialysis day), accumulation ratios for
metabolites were up to 1.8-fold and 1.4-fold for the dialysis day and non-dialysis day,
respectively, except for M13, for which accumulation ratio values were 2.6-fold and 1.5-fold,
respectively.
The efficacy and safety of daprodustat in patients with CKD on dialysis or not on dialysis is
primarily supported by the pivotal five global Phase 3 trials (ASCEND-ND, ASCEND-D,
ASCEND-TD, ASCEND-ID and ASCEND NHQ) in these populations. No dose adjustment is
needed for daprodustat in renal impairment.

8.2. Extrinsic Factors
Drug Interactions
Metabolic Pathway
In vitro studies demonstrated that daprodustat is primarily metabolized by CYP2C8 (95%
contribution), with a minor contribution of CYP3A4 (5% contribution). producing 6 predominant
circulating metabolites, of which M2, M3, M13, are major metabolites. (≥10%).

Jesduvroq (daprodustat)

Effect of Other Drugs on Daprodustat
Effect of Strong CYP2C8 Inhibitors on Daprodustat
The drug-drug interaction (DDI) of daprodustat with gemfibrozil, a strong CYP2C8 inhibitor,
was evaluated in a dedicated DDI study in healthy adults. Gemfibrozil 600 mg BID was dosed to
steady state and its effects were evaluated on a single-dose of daprodustat 100 mg. Coadministration of a single-dose 100 mg daprodustat with gemfibrozil resulted in a 3.92-fold and
18.6-fold increase in daprodustat Cmax and AUC(0-inf), respectively. This marked increase in both
rate and extent of systemic exposure of daprodustat when co-administered with gemfibrozil is
consistent with daprodustat being primarily metabolized by CYP2C8. The decrease in CYP2C8
metabolism of daprodustat is reflected by its ~4-fold increase in t½, 2.51 to 9.26 h when coadministered with gemfibrozil. Tmax was also delayed from 1.00 to 4.00 h.
Co-administration of daprodustat with strong CYP2C8 inhibitors (e.g., gemfibrozil) was
contraindicated in the global Phase 3 studies. The review team agrees with the Applicant that the
use of daprodustat should be contraindicated with strong CYP2C8 inhibitors in labeling.
Effect of Moderate CYP2C8 Inhibitors on Daprodustat
The Applicant did not conduct a dedicated in vivo DDI study of daprodustat with a moderate
CYP2C8 inhibitor. However, the phase 2/3 trials including ASCEND-D allowed the use of
clopidogrel, a moderate CYP2C8 inhibitor. The Applicant states that based on the Phase 2/3
PopPK analyses, co-administration of daprodustat with clopidogrel at steady state in 70 subjects
showed that clopidogrel increases the exposure of daprodustat by 1.59-fold (90% CI: 1.39-1.82).
PopPK analyses from previous Phase 2 and 3 clinical studies (GSK Reports 2019N405983_00
and 2019N405417_00) demonstrated up to 1.75- to 2.65-fold increase in daprodustat exposure
when co-administered with clopidogrel (n=54 Phase 2 and n=22 Japan Phase 3 subjects).
However, the Applicant’s current PopPK based DDI assessment is not adequate to accurately
characterize the effect of concomitant medications on the PK of daprodustat in the pivotal Phase
3 clinical trials due to the following limitations: (a) A binary covariate analysis was performed in
the Applicant’s PopPK modeling; (b) An adequate number of daprodustat PK samples during the
elimination phase were not available in the data from pivotal clinical trials; (c) There was a lack
of data on medication history prior to study entry and dosing records for concomitant
medications, such as, dose level, dose frequency, formulation, route of administration, start and
stop date and clock time, duration, dose modification, etc. in the PopPK dataset.
Physiological based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses showed that daprodustat AUC was
increased by approximately 7.6-fold following coadministration with a single dose of 300-mg
clopidogrel and approximately 4-fold following coadministration with maintenance doses of
clopidogrel (75 mg). However, the effects of clopidogrel on daprodustat may be underestimated
because the clopidogrel PBPK models used by the Applicant were not reliably validated and
these clopidogrel models significantly underpredicted the clinically observed interactions
between clopidogrel and repaglinide (see Section 14.6 for details). This suggests that the effect
of clopidogrel on daprodustat exposure is expected to be no less than the model-predicted
effects.

Although at least a 4-fold or higher increase in the AUC of daprodustat can be expected with
concomitant administration of clopidogrel, no obvious trends were observed in the number of Hb
increases per participant by baseline clopidogrel use, new clopidogrel use, or clopidogrel nonusers in pooled data from studies 200807/ASCEND-D and 200808/ASCEND-ND.
Figure 38 shows the mean evaluable hemoglobin and median assigned daprodustat dose by visit
and by clopidogrel co-administration at baseline in patients from 200807/ASCEND-D. The mean
Hb profile at each visit among clopidogrel users was similar to that in patients who are
clopidogrel non-users. While these results support concomitant clopidogrel use with daprodustat,
it is important to also compare daprodustat doses between clopidogrel users and non-users, since
daprodustat is titrated based on Hb response. Interestingly, there was no consistent trend for a
dose reduction of daprodustat in the clopidogrel users group compared to the non-users, as
observed from the closely overlaying median and the corresponding distribution bars for
daprodustat dose at each visit between the two groups, except for one step median dose decrease
at the second visit (Figure 38).

Although, a decrease in the daprodustat dose is expected based on the predicted 4-fold or higher
increase in exposure of daprodustat on concomitant clopidogrel administration, this was not
reflected in the clinical experience with daprodustat in the pivotal Phase 3 trial. The reason for
this observation is unclear, but the clinical experience from more than 100 patients who are
clopidogrel users does support concomitant use of clopidogrel with daprodustat. That said,
because of an expected 4-fold increase in daprodustat AUC on a background of clopidogrel at
75 mg, the review team recommends initiating clopidogrel users at a lower starting dose of

daprodustat to avoid exposure to high daprodustat concentrations during initiation except in
patients whose starting dose is already 1 mg, the lowest available strength of daprodustat. For
patients who may initiate clopidogrel while on daprodustat, the regular monitoring of Hb every
4 weeks should be adequate to inform the need for any dose adjustment of daprodustat.
Effect of CYP2C8 Inducers on Daprodustat
CYP2C8 is the primary CYP enzyme involved in the oxidative metabolism of daprodustat.
Concomitant administration of drugs that are inducers of CYP2C8 may decrease daprodustat
exposure. The Applicant proposed to monitor hemoglobin and adjust the dose of daprodustat as
appropriate when initiating or stopping therapy with CYP2C8 inducer. The review team agrees
with the Applicant’s proposal.
Effect of BCRP Inhibitors on Daprodustat
In vitro data indicate daprodustat is a substrate of the breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP)
(efflux ratio ~4) and therefore has the potential for in vivo drug interactions due to BCRPmediated inhibition (see Section 14.2 for details). BCRP inhibition-mediated drug interactions
are known to primarily occur at the intestine, affecting absorption, and at the liver bile
canalicular membrane, affecting hepatobiliary secretion. Oral daprodustat is well-absorbed, with
a calculated absorption fraction of 80%. Any intestinal BCRP inhibition would not be expected
to lead to a 20% increase in absorption. Daprodustat has limited hepatobiliary clearance and is
cleared primarily by oxidative metabolism. In the human ADME study following an IV infusion,
daprodustat only represented 0.5% of total radioactivity in the duodenal bile, with oxidation
metabolites accounting for the rest, and approximately 99.5% of the oral dose was excreted as
oxidation metabolites, with daprodustat accounting for 0.5%. Based on the disposition profile of
daprodustat, a clinically significant effect of BCRP inhibitors on daprodustat PK is not expected.
Effect of Acid Reducing Agents on Daprodustat
Daprodustat has a pH dependent solubility. Co-administration of daprodustat with acid reducing
agents could increase solubility and thus potentially increase the systemic exposure of
daprodustat. The Population PK model predicted up to 1.06-fold increase in Cmax and 0.996-fold
change in AUC of daprodustat with concomitant administration with acid reducing agents (N=
526). Because oral daprodustat is well-absorbed, with a calculated fraction absorbed of 80%, any
increase in absorption would not be expected to exceed 20%. A clinically significant effect of
acid reducing agents on daprodustat PK is not expected.
Effect of Phosphate Binders and Oral Iron on Daprodustat
There is a potential for commonly co-administered medications such as oral iron and phosphate
binders to affect the rate and extent of absorption of drugs including daprodustat. Coadministered oral iron (N=84) and phosphate binders (N=415), including sevelamer and
aluminum, calcium, magnesium, and bicarbonate-based binders, were tested as potential
covariates in the Phase 2/3 PopPK analysis. However, the Applicant’s current PopPK based DDI
assessment is not adequate to accurately characterize the effect of concomitant medications on
the PK of daprodustat in the pivotal Phase 3 clinical trials due to the limitations of analysis

method and concomitant medication dosing history and records as detailed under a previous
section discussing drug interaction with clopidogrel.
While a dedicated DDI study with oral iron or phosphate binders was not conducted, there is
clinical experience from ASCEND-D for concomitant use of daprodustat with phosphate binders.
Data from ASCEND-D shows that the phosphate binder user group in the daprodustat arm had a
median dose of 6 mg compared to a one-step lower dose of 4 mg for the non-users. This likely
reflects a potential interaction of daprodustat with phosphate binders per the expectation that a
decrease in daprodustat exposure could lead to a higher maintenance dose to maintain Hb in the
target range. That said, the extensive clinical experience from ASCEND-D supports the
concomitant use of phosphate binders with daprodustat as it is shown that regular Hb monitoring
and dose titration is adequate to manage a potential interaction. Although, a similar analysis has
not been conducted for concomitant administration of oral iron, the recommended hemoglobin
monitoring and the corresponding daprodustat dose titration algorithm seem adequate to support
concomitant administration of oral iron with daprodustat.

8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development
The Applicant submitted an initial pediatric study plan (iPSP) on April 6, 2016 and proposed a
waiver in children less than 1 year of age for daprodustat for the treatment of anemia associated
with chronic kidney disease due to uncertainty of the ability to demonstrate therapeutic benefit in
this age cohort. An amended agreed initial pediatric study plan letter was issued on May 24,
2021. The Division agreed with the Applicant’s request regarding deferring the start of the
clinical portion of the pediatric development program until after the adult population NDA has
been submitted.
The Applicant submitted a proposed pediatric study request on June 29, 2021, to conduct an
Integrated Pharmacokinetic and Safety Open-label Basket Trial of Daprodustat for the Treatment
of Anemia Associated with Chronic Kidney Disease in Male and Female Children and

8.4. Pregnancy, Lactation, and Females/Males of
Reproductive Potential
Animal Data
The following nonclinical data are used to support the label. Additional detailed information is
available in Section 13 for nonclinical data.
Table 112. Nonclinical Data Supporting Labeling on Fertility, Pregnancy, and Lactation
Labeling Section
Nonclinical Data
8.1 Pregnancy
Daprodustat was orally administered to pregnant rats at 0.5, 7, or 60 mg/kg/day
from gestation day 6 to gestation day 17 during the period of organogenesis. No
adverse effects were observed at doses less than or equal to 7 mg/kg/day
(3 times the MRHD based on body surface area). Daprodustat administration
resulted in post-implantation loss, increased embryofetal death, and reduction
in skeletal ossification in rats at a dose of 60 mg/kg/day (24 times the MRHD
based on body surface area), which was associated with maternal toxicity
(reduced body weight gain or weight loss). Maternal toxicity occurred at doses
associated with polycythemia.
Daprodustat was orally administered to pregnant rabbits at doses of 4, 30, or
60 mg/kg/day from gestation day 7 until gestation day 19 during the period of
organogenesis. No adverse effects were observed at doses less than or equal
to 30 mg/kg/day (24 times the MRHD based on body surface area).
Daprodustat administration was associated with low incidence of abortions and
fetal skeletal malformations (irregularly shaped anterior fontanelle, manubrium,
fused sternal centra, and microphthalmia) at a dose of 60 mg/kg/day (49 times
the MRHD based on body surface area) in the presence of maternal toxicity
(reduced body weight gain or weight loss) and polycythemia.
In a pre- and postnatal development study, pregnant rats were dosed orally with
daprodustat from implantation until weaning (gestation day 6 to lactation day
21) at 0.8, 7, or 40 mg/kg/day concomitantly with 3 major human metabolites of
daprodustat. No adverse effects were observed at doses less than or equal to
7 mg/kg/day (3 times the MRHD based on body surface area). Maternal toxicity
was noted at 40 mg/kg/day, which represents 16-times the MRHD based on
body surface area, which was associated with dystocia and increased pup
deaths and decreased pup weights.

Labeling Section
8.2 Lactation

13.1 Carcinogenesis,
Mutagenesis,
Impairment of Fertility

Nonclinical Data
In a pre- and postnatal development study in rats, when daprodustat was orally
administered to maternal rats during the lactation period, the drug was detected
in plasma of suckling pups on postnatal day 10. The plasma concentration of
daprodustat in pups was 2.3% to 3.7% of daprodustat detected in the plasma of
dams when dosed at 40 mg/kg/day.
Daprodustat was not carcinogenic in two-year carcinogenicity studies when
administered orally at doses of 0.02, 0.1, 0.8, or 4 mg/kg/day (males)/
7 mg/kg/day (females) in rats and 0.2, 0.8, or 3 mg/kg/day (including
subcutaneous injection of major human metabolites of daprodustat) in mice.
Daprodustat was negative for mutagenic or clastogenic potential in the in vitro
bacterial reverse mutation assay, the in vitro human lymphocyte chromosomal
aberration assay, and the in vivo rat bone marrow micronucleus assay.
In a fertility and early embryonic development study in rats, daprodustat was
administered orally at doses of 2, 7, or 100 mg/kg/day in females, resulting in
maternal toxicity of reduced body weight gain and decreased mean uterine
weight, associated with decreased number of corpora lutea, implantations, and
live fetuses, and increased post-implantation loss at the dose of 100 mg/kg/day
(41 times the MRHD based on body surface area).

Source: FDA Reviewer
Abbreviations: MRHD, maximum recommended human dose

Details of above information and dose multiples shown are based on body surface area compared
between animals and humans.
Human Data
The Division of Pediatric and Maternal Health (DPMH) was consulted for advice on labeling
text for Section 8.1 to 8.3 (Pregnancy and Lactation Labeling).
There are limited human pregnancy outcome data for daprodustat from the Sponsor’s
Pharmacovigilance Database. There were 9 pregnancies exposed to daprodustat identified in the
clinical trials. The outcomes for these 9 pregnancies were




7 spontaneous abortions (SABs) at ages 6 to19 weeks gestation (including both fetuses of
one twin pregnancy)
2 elective abortions
One normal live birth

There was no human pregnancy outcome data found in the published English language literature.
The findings from animal studies suggest potential for fetal harm from exposure to daprodustat.
Labeling will note that CKD is associated with adverse pregnancy outcomes, that animal data
suggest harm, and that there are inadequate human pregnancy data.
Pregnancy would be expected to be uncommon in women in dialysis and a traditional pregnancy
registry or a claims database study would likely be unsuccessful due to low enrollment.
Therefore, DPMH recommends a PMR Descriptive Pregnancy Safety Study to follow-up on
maternal and infant outcomes of pregnancies that do occur. Refer to Section 24 for a detailed
summary of the PMR.
DPMH also recommends that the Applicant submit annual reports of daprodustat utilization rates
amongst females of reproductive potential (females aged 15 to 50 years) calculated cumulatively
199
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
[To complete this review, detailed instructions are available in the Integrated Review Template How-to Guide.]
Reference ID: 5119138

NDA 216951
Jesduvroq (daprodustat)

from the time of initial approval. If there appears to be substantial use of daprodustat in females
of reproductive potential, then this would be considered new safety information that would
trigger FDAAA and would result in the Agency issuing a PMR for a pregnancy registry study at
that time.
There are no data on the presence of daprodustat in human milk, its effect on a breastfed infant
or milk production. Parameters such as daprodustat’s low molecular weight (~393 Daltons) and
the presence of daprodustat in rat milk suggest it is likely that daprodustat will be present in
human milk. The high protein binding (99%) suggest that the amount present is likely to be low.
However, given the spectrum of serious adverse events that can be seen with daprodustat in
adults, breastfeeding is not recommended during treatment with daprodustat and for one month
after the last dose.
Given the low incidence of pregnancy and live births in the proposed population and
recommendation against use during breastfeeding, a PMR for a lactation study would not be
feasible and the alternative of performing a lactation study in healthy volunteers would not be
ethically permissible in view of DPMH because a single dose of daprodustat could potentially
cause thrombosis in a predisposed population of recently pregnant/locating women.
There were no clinical studies of the adverse effect of daprodustat on fertility. Recommendations
for pregnancy testing or recommendations for contraception while taking daprodustat would not
be appropriate given that the fetal harm warning is based on adverse effects in a single animal
species at multiples greater than 10 times the maximum recommend human dose. Therefore,
DPMH recommends that subsection 8.3 be omitted from the labeling.
For additional details regarding the DPMH review, refer to the consult review by Jane Liedkta
M.D, Tamrar Johnson MD, MS and Lynne P. Yao, MD.

9. Product Quality
The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) review team has assessed NDA 216951 with
respect to chemistry, manufacturing, and controls (CMC) and has determined that it meets all
applicable standards to support the identity, strength, quality, and purity that it purports. As such
OPQ recommends approval of this NDA from a quality perspective.
For additional details, refer to the OPQ Integrated Quality Assessment dated December 28, 2022.

Investigators were trained to conduct the study in accordance with GCPs and the study protocol,

conduct the study in accordance with ICH GCP and all applicable subject privacy requirements,
and the ethical principles that are outlined in the Declaration of Helsinki, and to conduct the
study in accordance with the protocol.
The study was monitored in accordance with ICH E6, Section 5.18. If significant findings (e.g.,
potential serious misconduct or noncompliance with GCP, including potential Serious Breaches)
were identified during monitoring or auditing of a site, these are presented in Section 5.2,
Protocol Deviations, of this report.

11. Advisory Committee Summary
The Division of Non-Malignant Hematology (DNH) convened a virtual advisory committee
meeting to discuss daprodustat on October 26, 2022. DNH sought the advisory committee’s
advice and recommendations on the safety issues discussed in this review and whether the
benefits of daprodustat outweigh its risks in both the non-dialysis and dialysis populations.
The advisory committee members voted 5 yes vs. 11 no on the question of whether the benefits
outweigh the risks for anemia of chronic kidney disease in patients not on dialysis. For the
dialysis population, the vote was 13 yes vs. 3 no. Committee members recognized that the oral
route of administration would provide convenience to some patients and another treatment
option. However, the committee was concerned about the potential further increase in some
cardiovascular risks seen only in the non-dialysis population when compared to the
erythropoietin stimulating agent control, which itself carries these risks. See the Minutes and
Transcript available on the external FDA website (fda.gov)20 for further details. Our actions on

20 https://www.fda.gov/advisory-committees/advisory-committee-calendar/updated-contact-information-october-26-

2022-meeting-cardiovascular-and-renal-drugs-advisory

III. Additional Analyses and Information
12. Summary of Regulatory History
Daprodustat was granted approval for the treatment of adults with anemia due to chronic kidney
disease (CKD) on or not on dialysis on June 29, 2020, in Japan.
Investigational New Drug Application IND 101291 was received on June 3, 2008, to study
GSK1278863 (also referred to as daprodustat) for the treatment for anemia of chronic kidney
disease. The initial IND included the protocol titled PHX111427: A Phase I, Randomized,
Single-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Dose-Escalation Study to Evaluate the Safety,
Pharmacokinetics, and Pharmacodynamics of Single Oral Doses of GSK1278863A in Healthy
Subjects. The IND became active on July 3, 2008.
Transfer of IND 102191 ownership from GlaxoSmithKline to Octagon Research Solutions, Inc.
was made on February 4, 2009. The ownership was transferred back to GlaxoSmithKline on
March 1, 2011.
A Type C End of Phase 2A meeting was held on July 23, 2012, to discuss questions related to
dosing. In addition, the Applicant proposed to conduct separate phase 3 trials to assess efficacy
in two groups – patients requiring hemodialysis (HDD) and patients not requiring hemodialysis
(non-dialysis dependent [NDD]), and planned to assess cardiovascular safety in a prospectively
defined meta-analysis across their phase 3 trials. In terms of dosing, the Agency stated the
Applicant’s approach seemed reasonable and encouraged them to consider requesting another
End of Phase 2A meeting with the Agency to discuss the details of their dosing modeling
approaches to select the appropriate dose for their phase 3 trials. The Agency also commented
that separate meta-analyses should be performed for NDD and HDD patients, instead of
combining both populations, and recommended the Applicant to consider a superiority design for
cardiovascular safety.
A carcinogenicity Special Protocol Assessment request was submitted for the proposed
carcinogenicity protocol entitled GSK1278863: A 2-year Oral (Gavage) Carcinogenicity Study
in Rats on August 16, 2013, and an agreement letter was issued on September 20, 2013. Another
carcinogenicity Special Protocol Assessment request was submitted for the proposed
carcinogenicity protocol entitled GSK1278863: A 2 Year Carcinogenicity Study in Mice on
August 27, 2014, and an agreement letter was issued on October 8, 2014.
The Applicant submitted various meeting requests to obtain feedback and guidance in
preparation for submission of a new marketing application for daprodustat. An End of Phase 2
meeting was held on February 22, 2016, to discuss the non-clinical and clinical program
conducted to date and the phase 3 clinical development plan for daprodustat in the context of
supporting a future NDA submission for daprodustat.

13. Pharmacology Toxicology
13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With
the Investigational New Drug Application
13.1.1. Primary pharmacology
Daprodustat is a reversible inhibitor of hypoxia-inducible factor (HIF)-PH1, PH2, and PH3
which results in the stabilization and nuclear accumulation of HIF-1α and HIF-2α transcription
factors, leading to increased transcription of the HIF-responsive genes, including erythropoietin.
Treatment of daprodustat in vitro and in vivo pharmacology studies showed that daprodustat
increases erythropoietin (EPO) levels (mRNA and protein), which results in an increase of red
blood cell (RBC) parameters. Tabulated summary of the in vitro and in vivo pharmacology
studies in animals conducted in support of daprodustat’s mechanism of action are listed below.
Daprodustat was coded as GSK1278863A in the pharmacology study reports.
Table 114. Summary of the In Vitro and In Vivo Pharmacology Studies
Study/ Study No.
Findings
Mechanism of action:
Biochemical Activity of the The effects of daprodustat on in vitro enzymatic activity of HIF PH3 and PH2,
Prolyl Hydroxylase
referred to as egg-laying deficiency protein nine-like protein 3 and 1 (EGLN3
Inhibitor GSK1278863A/ and EGLN1) in the study report, were measured using a LANCE™

(Homogeneous Time-Resolved Fluorescence Quenching Assay based on

To evaluate the selectivity of daprodustat, two close homolog PHs, αketoglutarate dependent iron dioxygenases CP4H and factor inhibiting HIF

Daprodustat competitively inhibited enzymatic activity of HIF-prolylhydroxylases PH3 and PH2 (Ki=1.8 and 7.3nM, respectively).
Daprodustat inhibited PHD3 with reversibility in a time dependent manner.
The dissociation half-life (t1/2) of daprodustat was 132 min, suggesting that
daprodustat does not instantaneously dissociate and prolonged effect of
daprodustat is possible.

data are derived from 11-point concentration-response curves. PH2, PH1,

Normal female B6D2F1 mice were dosed with 60 mg/kg daprodustat, and
blood samples were collected (n=5/timepoint) at 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, and
16 hours after administration of daprodustat (vehicle controls collected at the
4, 8, and 12 hour timepoints) via oral gavage. To evaluate the effect of
daprodustat on EPO protein level in plasma, PPP samples were prepared
and analyzed by ELISA. Additionally, liver and kidneys were collected at the
2, 4, 6, 8, 10, and 12 hours after administration timepoints to evaluate EPO
and PGK1 mRNA expression using TaqMan RT-PCR (normalized to
GAPDH).

Daprodustat treatment at 60 mg/kg in mouse induced EPO liver mRNA
expression and plasma protein levels exhibited a biphasic response with
peaks at 8 and 12 hours after dosing. EPO liver mRNA expression increased
from 279-fold (relative to vehicle control) observed at 2 hours after dosing to
449-fold at the 6-hour time point. Liver EPO mRNA expression then
decreased to 31-fold at the 8-hour time point, then was increased again at
the 12-hour timepoint (279-fold; 10-hour timepoint was not evaluated).
Kidney EPO mRNA expression was increased by 56-fold and 35-fold,
respectively, at the 8-hour and 12-hour timepoints. PGK1 expression was
minimally affected by daprodustat-treatment.

mechanism of action, secondary pharmacology studies were conducted screening for the activity
in vitro of daprodustat on a range of binding uptake sites expressed on tissues/organs.

Findings
To investigate any potential inhibitory activity of daprodustat for the
protein kinase enzymes, daprodustat was evaluated against
cyclooxygenase 1 and 2 enzymes (COX1 and COX2) and 7 protein
kinases [CDK-2/Cyclin A, PLK1, 70 kDa ribosomal protein S6 kinase I
(p70S6K), SRC1, DYRK3 (also known as YAK3), VEGFR-2 and rat
RSK1].
Daprodustat weakly and reversibly inhibited COX1 activity with an IC50
value of 66 μM, which suggests low likelihood of functional inhibition
in vivo. Daprodustat had no effect on COX2 activity. It is unlikely that
the COX1 inhibition by daprodustat is directly contributing to the
adverse effects on stomach, considering the unbound concentration
of daprodustat needed to inhibit COX1 by 50% is 764-fold higher than
the maximum free drug concentration (Cmax) at the maximum
recommended human dose.
The IC50 values for all protein kinases tested were >10µM which
suggests that daprodustat selectively inhibits PHs (with lower IC50
values in nM range) vs. the protein kinases.

kinase-1; SRC1, steroid receptor coactivator 1; VEGFR-2, vascular endothelial growth factor receptor-2; YAK3, Dual specificity
tyrosine phosphorylation-regulated kinase 3

13.1.3. Safety Pharmacology




Daprodustat had no effect on hERG channel activity (up to 47.66µM), neurobehavioral,
and respiratory activity (up to 20 mg/kg).
Mild cardiovascular effects of daprodustat including small magnitude of QT and QRS
prolongation was observed in ex vivo study with rabbit left ventricular wedge at
concentrations ≥10µM.
Mild reversible increase in heart rate was observed in rats after repeated oral dosing of
daprodustat for 5 days at doses of ≥30 mg/kg and did not alter the mean arterial pressure.

13.1.4. ADME/PK
Absorption
Pharmacokinetics of daprodustat (coded as GSK1278863A or GSK1278863 in the
pharmacokinetic study reports) and its predominant human metabolites were evaluated in mice,
rats, dogs, and monkeys. For the species that do not generate the metabolites (mice and rats), the
animals were treated with major human metabolites of daprodustat [GSK2391220 (M2),
GSK2506104 (M3), and GSK2531401(M13)].


Single dose studies were conducted with daprodustat and its metabolites in mouse, rat,
dog, and monkeys for characterization of pharmacokinetics of daprodustat.

due to lack of precision in the assay as the % bound approaches 100%.

Abbreviations: Cb/Cp, concentration in blood to concentration in plasma ratio

Blood to Plasma Ratio




The blood to plasma and blood to liver ratios were tested in Sprague Dawley rats with a
single oral dose of 10 mg/kg [14C]-radiolabeled daprodustat (CD2008/00100). The
highest mean concentrations of radioactivity of blood, plasma, and liver (37.4-76.1 and
22.2-30.7 µg equiv/g, respectively) were observed at 1 hour post dose in males and
females. Low association of radioactivity with blood cells were noted with blood to
plasma ratio 0.505-0.560 and mean liver to blood ratios (0.319-0.825) decreased through
24 hours post dose.
The blood to plasma ratio was 0.84 µg equiv/g in dogs (intact) (CD2008/00025) and the
average blood to plasma concentration ratios were 0.64 µg equiv/g for both intact and
BDC monkeys.

Whole Body Distribution


In the Long Evans rat, the tissue radioactivity was evaluated with [14C]-radiolabeled
daprodustat (CD2008/00271/00) and the result exhibited that daprodustat was widely
distributed into the tissues tested (CNS [Brain, choroid plexus, meninges, peripheral
nerve, and spinal cord], glands [adrenal, pineal, pituitary, thyroid, lachrymal, harderian,
pancreas, salivary], fat [brown or abdominal], skin [non-pigmented or pigmented],
reproductive organs [prostate gland, epididymis, and testis], skeletal muscle [skeletal and
heart], respiratory tract [lung and nasal turbinates], GI tract mucosa [esophagus, cecum,
small/large intestine, and stomach], and ocular tissue [lens and uveal tract]).
- The highest radioactivity concentrations at any timepoint were observed in the GI
tract. For most non-GI tract tissues, the highest radioactivity concentrations were
observed at 1 h, including in the blood, aorta, lung, and meninges.
- The radioactivity concentrations were low in the brain suggesting little penetration of
the blood brain barrier.
- The only radioactivity detected by Day 7 was low levels observed in peripheral
nerves and large intestine contents.
- Radioactivity concentrations in the uveal tract, pigmented skin, and meninges peak at
1h or 4h declining steadily afterwards until undetectable by Day 7. This finding
suggests no irreversible binding to melanin-containing tissues.

Metabolism



Intrinsic clearance of daprodustat was low in the liver microsomes and hepatocytes of
mouse, rat, dog, monkey, and human based on clearance (CLi) data (UH2008/0018).
Total of 19 metabolites of daprodustat were identified, of which 14 metabolites were
detected in human hepatocytes.

Figure 40. Possible Metabolic Pathway of Daprodustat

Source: Applicant’s submission









Daprodustat is metabolized mainly to products of di- and tri-oxidation via hydroxylation
of the cyclohexane rings in humans.
The major human metabolites [>10% of DRM] of daprodustat detected in patients
include M2 (GSK2391220), M3 (GSK2531403 and GSK2506104), and M13
(GSK2531400 and GSK2531401), of which M3 and M13 exist as enantiomers. The
predominant circulating stereoisomers of the major human metabolites M3 and M13 are
GSK2506401 and GSK2531401, respectively.
The 3 major circulating human metabolites (M2, M3, and M13) were present in rabbit
and monkey plasma with an animal:human AUC ratio of at least 1X in monkeys
(comparing the 50 mg/kg MTD dose in the 39-week toxicity study and human exposure
at the 48 mg three times a week (TIW) human dose).
The metabolites were not detected in mouse, rat, or dog plasma, and were administered
SC as a metabolite cocktail to mice and rats in a mouse carcinogenicity study and rat preand post-natal development study to achieve at least 1X human exposure.
The intrinsic clearance values for daprodustat were low (<0.5-1.1 mL/min/g liver) in liver
microsomes and hepatocytes of all species examined including mouse, rat, dog, monkey,
and human. (UH2008/00018/00).

Excretion
Single oral administrations of [14C] daprodustat to intact male and female rats and dogs and to
male monkeys, and to BDC male rats, dogs, and monkeys (CD2008/00100, CD2008/00025, and
2018N355713) showed that feces is the major route of elimination of daprodustat in intact
animals. Urinary and biliary recoveries indicated that they are minor routes of elimination and
the highest blood radioactivity concentrations were observed at 1 hour after dosing, and the
majority of recovered radioactivity was excreted within the first 24 hours.

13.1.5. Toxicology
13.1.5.1. General Toxicology
General toxicology studies of 4- and 13-weeks duration were conducted in Sprague Dawley rats
and Beagle Dogs with rats receiving daily oral doses by gavage and dogs by capsule. A 13-week
toxicity study was also conducted in the cynomolgus monkey with daily dosing by oral gavage.
Toxicities in rats were overall consistent with toxicities observed in the 26-week rat study and
were mainly due to exaggerated pharmacology (see Section 13.2). Higher doses were tolerated in
the 4- and 13-week rat studies compared to the 26-week rat study. The 100 mg/kg/day dose
administered to rats in the 13-week study toxicity resulted in severe toxicities to the heart
(valvulopathy, atrial thrombus, valvular thrombus, endocardial hyperplasia, and
cardiomyopathy), lung (chronic passive congestion), kidney (infarct, thrombus, cortical tubular
pigment and tubular basophilia), brain (focal degeneration) and stomach (glandular
erosion/ulceration, submucosal inflammatory cell infiltrate, hemorrhage, and vascular medial
hypertrophy). Multifocal erosions, ulcers, hemorrhage, edema, and/or inflammation in the
glandular region of the stomach were observed at doses as low as 7 mg/kg/day in the 4-week
study. Toxicities in dogs were due to the exaggerated pharmacology of daprodustat related to
polycythemia and hyperviscosity of the blood leading to poor tissue perfusion and anoxia. In
monkeys, there were fewer organs of toxicity (only bone marrow [erythroid hypercellulartity],
lung [alveolar hemorrhage], thymus [lymphoid atrophy], and stomach [hemorrhage and effects

[To complete this review, detailed instructions are available in the Integrated Review Template How-to Guide.]
Reference ID: 5119138

Jesduvroq (daprodustat)

on body weight and clinical signs/chemistry]) in the 13-week study compared to the 39-week
study, but the toxicities in the 13-week study were more severe at the higher dose tested of
100 mg/kg/day (see Section 13.2).

Key Study Findings
Sprague Dawley male rats (n=3/group) were administered 2 oral doses of
1000 or 2000 mg/kg, approximately 24-hours apart.
The mean % proportion of immature polychromatic erythrocytes (PCE) was
reduced by ~23% at ≥1000 mg/kg/day daprodustat. In addition, a statistically
significant 2-fold increase in micronucleated PCE (MPCE) frequency that
exceeded the upper 98% tolerance limit of historical controls was observed in
the 2000 mg/kg/day daprodustat group. There was also a slight increase
(approximately 1.6-fold) in group mean reticulocyte count observed in
treatment groups compared to the control group. The result was positive, but
it is not considered to be a direct genotoxic response based on the small
magnitude of the response and the mechanism of action of GSK1278863A
which can contribute to the positive result.

EPO and VEGF levels in plasma were not increased with single or 3 daily
oral doses of daprodustat. Although, the EPO mRNA expression in liver was
dose-dependently increased at ≥10 mg/kg 6 hours post-dose (up to 324X vs.
control). HO-1 mRNA expression (up to 18X vs. control), was also increased
at 30 mg/kg, 6 hours post-dose.

GSK2391220A,

GSK1278863):
Subcutaneous Repeat
Dose Pharmacodynamic

in Female Mice

No. M70590N)/
2014N208198

To evaluate the effect of major metabolites of daprodustat on erythropoiesis,
female ICR mice (n=10/group) were treated subcutaneously once daily for
28 days with a metabolite cocktail consisting of GSK2391220A (M2),
GSK2506104A (M3) and GSK2531401A (M13) at 0 (vehicle of 25mM
phosphate buffer, pH 7.4), 2.0, 2.5, and 1.3 mg/kg/day, respectively.
Metabolite cocktail treatment resulted in increased RBC parameters (RBC,
hemoglobin, and hematocrit) by ~10% compared to control on Day 29. The
result shows that the exposure is achieved for the metabolites which are
comparable to clinical exposure and the metabolite cocktail has
pharmacology effect on their own with delayed response compared to the
parent compound.

13.2.3. General Toxicology
Title/ Study No.: GSK1278863A: A 26-week Oral (Gavage) Toxicity Study in the Rat/
2011N126130
Key Study Findings







Daprodustat (GSK1278663A) produced drug-related mortality at 10 mg/kg/day. Seven
main study and four toxicokinetic (TK) males were found dead or euthanized. Due to
mortality and toxicity observed at 10 mg/kg/day, all surviving males in this group were
pre-terminally euthanized during Week 20 (Day 135/134). Cause of death appeared
related to hemorrhage, systemic thrombosis and/or necrosis and thrombosis of various
organs including the heart, lung, kidney, and cecum. These findings are consistent with
the drug’s primary pharmacology and secondary impact on hemodynamics.
Moderately higher reticulocyte (~70% ↑) and red blood cell counts (29-41% ↑) were
observed by week 26/27 in males and females at 10 mg/kg/day. Females could tolerate
the elevations (reticulocytes up to 82% ↑, red blood cell counts 39% ↑), but males could
not.
Systemic thrombosis, hemorrhage, and necrosis were observed at 10 mg/kg/day and in
the heart and lung of males at 10 mg/kg/day.
The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) was determined at 4 mg/kg/day based
on the mortality and drug-related toxic effects observed at 10 mg/kg/day.

Clinical signs

The primary daprodustat-related clinical sign was skin reddening of the extremities
(forelimb, hindlimb, pinna, tail, and/or muzzle). HD animals started presenting skin
reddening as early as Week 6 in males and Week 7 in females and most of the HD
animals presented skin reddening by Week 12. Skin reddening also occurred in
males given 4 or 0.8 mg/kg/day, starting at Weeks 10 and 11, respectively.

Body weights
and Food

Major Findings
HD males exhibited decreased mean body weight gain (33% ↓ compared to controls)
from approximately Week 9 until their early termination in Week 20 with

Figure 43. Body Weight Changes of Rats With Daprodustat Treatment for 26
Weeks (Top: Males, Bottom: Females)

Source: Applicant’s submission

Ophthalmoscopy Congestion of retinal vessels were observed in HD females (5/12) in Week 25 and
the ophthalmic evaluation was not conducted in HD males due to early termination.
Hematology
 Daprodustat-related findings includes increase of red blood cell (RBC) mass
(RBCs, hemoglobin, and hematocrit), reticulocytes, and neutrophils and
decrease of platelet number.
 The increase of RBC parameters peaks at Week 13 and the increase remains
through Week 26/27. The hematology of HD males was not evaluated at the
end of dosing due to premature termination.
 HD females had elevated hematocrit but no mortality occurred which suggests
females have higher tolerance to daprodustat-related effect on hemodynamics.
 Platelet number decreased at ≥MD which is associated with the mechanism of
action of daprodustat. Overexpression of HIF1-α is known to result in low
platelet number

Parameter
Serum cardiac
troponin

Major Findings
At doses of 0.8 and 4 mg/kg/day, increased serum troponin (cTnI) values in individual
treated males at Week 13 were between 3.7- and 5.8-fold, relative to the highest
concurrent control value of 0.051 μg/L. On Week 26, serum cTnI values in males
ranged from 2.3- to 14-fold, relative to the highest concurrent control value of 0.026
μg/L. The increases in cTnI were not dose-related and all rats with serum cTnI values
greater than 0.028 μg/L had microscopic evidence of cardiomyopathy, a background
finding in rats.
ECG
Not conducted.
Clinical chemistry Daprodustat-related effects include dose-dependent increase of triglycerides, iron
and total iron binding capacity and decrease of cholesterol.
 During week 26/27, one female (Animal No. 4504) at 10 mg/kg/day had
elevated ALT (613 U/L) and AST (501 U/L), a 6- and 3-fold increase compared
to the control mean, respectively, that were associated with microscopic
findings of decreased liver weight and extramedullary hematopoiesis.
 Potassium (K) concentrations were decreased in males and females at
10 mg/kg/day during Week 4 (down to 0.81X) and at ≥ 4 mg/kg/day (down to
0.86X) during Week 13, which seems to be a daprodustat effect on HIF.

Urinalysis

Gross pathology

Significant increases (59%) in creatine and glucose (102%) were observed in the
urine of females in the 10 mg/kg/day group (compared to control females) during
week 4, which normalized thereafter.
Daprodustat-related findings include dark discoloration, clots, and/or dark fluid in the
abdomen, adrenal glands, digestive content, eye, GI tract, kidneys, liver, lymph
nodes, pancreas, prostate (males), skeletal muscle, thorax, trachea, urinary bladder,
thymus and/or whole body, mostly observed at HD. These findings correspond with
histopathological findings of congestion/hemorrhage in multiple organs.
HD males presented dilatation of blood vessels/heart chambers (1/12) and spongy
and uncollapsed lungs (6/12) which corresponds to microscopic finding of congestion.
In addition, splenomegaly (4/12, corresponds to extramedullary hematopoiesis),

enlarged adrenal glands (2/12, corresponds to cortical hypertrophy), and

no organ weight data available). A decrease in liver weights in both sexes was
observed, which is aligned with the microscopic finding of atrophy at HD males.
Spleen weights decreased in LD and MD males but spleen weight increase was
noted in HD females.

Histopathology
Adequate

Most of the histological findings related to daprodustat-treatment were noted in HD

Title/ Study No.: GSK1278863A: A 39-week Oral (Gavage) Toxicity Study in the Cynomolgus Monkey with a
6-week Recovery Period /2011N126131

Key Study Findings










One mortality (unscheduled termination due to moribundity) occurred at LD but the
cause of death is not determined.
Lower body weight gain was noted in all daprodustat-treated groups, but the findings
resolved after the recovery period.
Daprodustat-related increase of RBC mass, reticulocytes, and serum iron parameters at
≥10 mg/kg/day was observed, related to the direct pharmacological effect of daprodustat,
and corresponding evidence of erythropoiesis were noted including congestion in
multiple organs (kidneys, liver, brain, and eye) or whole body, skin reddening, and bone
marrow erythroid hypercellularity.
Other drug-related microscopic findings include gastric hemorrhage, erosions and/or
ulcerations in the stomach, pigment deposits in kidney, mononuclear cell infiltration of
choroid plexus, and degeneration of skeletal muscle, and these findings persisted after the
recovery period.
One high dose (HD) female presented partly closed right eye (ptosis of the right upper
eyelid) and anisocoria (mydriasis of the right pupil) and congestion of the retinal vessels
at ≥mid dose and optic disc hyperemia at HD were observed at the end of dosing period
which persisted with decreased incidence/severity at the end of recovery period.
The NOAEL is determined at 3 mg/kg/day based on the microscopic findings in choroid
plexus, kidney, and stomach at ≥10 mg/kg/day.

Table 139. Observations and Results of the 39-Week Oral (Gavage) Toxicity Study in the
Cynomolgus Monkey With a 6-Week Recovery Period /2011N126131
Parameter
Major Findings
Mortality
One LD male was terminated due to moribundity on Day 262 (Week 38). Diarrhea and
liquid feces, significant body weight loss and changes in clinical chemistry (decreased
neutrophil count, decreased urea, albumin, total protein, total calcium, phosphorus
and cholesterol concentrations, increased potassium concentrations) were consistent
with protein loss and poor clinical condition. The cause of death could not be
determined at the time of necropsy. This animal had bone marrow hypocellularity,
thickening of the wall of the small intestine with moderate muscular
hypertrophy/hyperplasia in the jejunum, mild multifocal acinar cell atrophy in the
pancreas, marked thymus lymphoid atrophy, and minimal fibrosis of the lung and
spleen. TK analysis shows that this animal was not exposed to higher levels of test
article compared to other animals in the group. Metabolite levels were not measured in
this animal. This mortality appears unrelated to the test article (lack of dose response
and comparable findings to animals at higher doses).
Clinical signs
Daprodustat related findings were noted:
 Skin reddening, primarily on the cranium, with dose dependence was observed
in all monkeys with higher incidence. Other affected locations include but are not
limited to: gums and/or mouth, fore limbs, hind limbs, abdominal area and
ventral thoracic and/or cervical region. Scabby skin was more frequently
observed in higher number of HD males.
 Dose-related changes in feces (soft and/or liquid, color change to green/pale)
was noted and liquid vagina discharge was observed in females treated with
daprodustat.
 Red skin at the right periorbital area and right eyelid, partly closed eyes (right
eye, ptosis and mydriasis) was observed in one HD female (No. 453) between
dosing Days 138-149. Eye position, movements and pupillary light reflexes were
normal. These observations led to the hypothesis of an oculomotor nerve palsy
supported by the histological brain findings (localized inflammation [superficial
brainstem/nerve root] and concurrent changes including edema and
hemorrhage). Findings were fully or partially reversible.

Coagulation

Dose-dependent increase in PT and APTT were observed at the end of dosing period
which resolved after the recovery period. These changes in coagulation factors are
indirectly drug-related, which may be linked to the decreased platelet counts, high
hematocrit and over-citrated plasma observed in daprodustat-treated groups.

Chemistry

No daprodustat-related effects were observed. Cardiac troponin levels were measured

No daprodustat-related changes were observed
 Decreased total iron concentration at Weeks 4 and 13 at HD with increase of

the pharmacological effects of daprodustat. Findings were reversible and during

decreased food consumption and stomach lesion (hemorrhage and erosion),

dosing period at ≥ MD and showed trend of recovery after the recovery period.
This finding may reflect liver damage and corresponds to the findings of slight
increase of liver weight, fibrosis and dark discoloration at HD.
 Creatinine concentrations were increased which corresponds to the
macro/microscopic findings in kidney, and it resolved after the recovery period.

Organ weights

Histopathology
Adequate
battery: Yes

Daprodustat-related changes in organ weights (%, relative to body weight) were
noted. Increased organ weights of heart up to 21% and 19%, kidney up to 17% and
15%, liver up to 20-24% were noted at HD in males and females, respectively. The
affected organs are associated with daprodustat-related hematopoiesis. Recovery or
trend of recovery were noted at the end of recovery period.
Daprodustat-related findings were noted in multiple tissues/organs including brain,
bone marrow, kidneys, stomach, and the whole body. Majority of findings are likely
related to pharmacological effect of daprodustat increasing hematocrit.
Increased hypercellularity of bone marrow and iron deposition in kidney are
associated with direct pharmacological effect of daprodustat. Findings in the brain
(mononuclear cell infiltration in the choroid plexus and subacute inflammation, edema,
and hemorrhage in the superficial brainstem) and stomach (hemorrhage and
ulceration of glandular mucosa) are considered daprodustat-related adverse findings
which showed trend of recovery but persisted after the recovery period.

Toxicokinetics

The systemic exposure of daprodustat based on the Cmax and AUC increased with
dose increment with less than dose-proportional manner. The sex difference was less
than 2-fold between male and female and the tmax ranged from 0.5 to 8 hours.
The metabolites of daprodustat [GSK2391220 (M2), GSK2531403 (M3), GSK2499166
(M4), GSK2531399 (M5), GSK2531398 (M6) and GSK2531400 (M13)] were detected
in plasma in all drug-treated groups with increased exposure with dose increment and
the metabolite to parent ratios exposures (AUC) were less than 0.09. The maximum
plasma concentrations for the metabolites were generally observed between 1 and 8
hours after dosing during Week 39.

for 39 Weeks

NDA 216951
Jesduvroq (daprodustat)



Daprodustat had no effect on reproductive potential in male Sprague Dawley rats up to

GLP Compliance: Yes
Title/ Study Number: GSK1278863A: Oral Female Fertility and Early Embryonic Development Study in Rats/
CD2009/00236/00

Key Study Findings





Maternal toxicity at HD of 100 mg/kg/day includes significant decrease in body weight
gain (BWG) during the premating period of Day 0-14 (80%) and up to gestation Day 7
(28%) with corresponding food consumption decrease up to 15% compared to controls.
In HD females, statistically significant decrease of mean uterine weight (19%), number of
corpora lutea (14%), number of implantations (15%), number of live fetuses (21%), and a
marked increase of total postimplantation loss were observed.
There were no effects on estrous cycles, days needed for mating, and mating and fertility
indices up to 100 mg/kg/day.
NOAEL for maternal toxicity, female fertility, and embryo-fetal development in rats is
7 mg/kg/day based on the GSK1278863A-related adverse findings in HD.

Major Findings
Red and/or clear material around mouth and nose were observed within 15
min or 1 hour following dose administration beginning on study Day 8 and
continuing until GD 6 in multiple females at 100 mg/kg/day
Premating BWG, Day 0-14: Dose-dependent reduced mean BWG (13, 20,

Corresponding decrease in food consumption (12- to %) were observed

stopped.

lutea (14%), number of implantation sites (15%), and number of live fetuses
(21%) were observed with statistical significance at 100 mg/kg/day.

resorptions) increased by >200% at 100 mg/kg/day.

malformations/developmental variations observed:

13.2.4.2. Embryo-Fetal Development
Title/ Study Number: GSK1278863A: Oral (Gavage) Once Daily Embryo Fetal Development Study in the
Crl:CD(SD) Rat/ CD2009/00388/01 (aka Study No. AFA00832)

Key Study Findings






Maternal toxicity at the HD of 60 mg/kg/day (significant decreases in body weight gain
(BWG; -21% overall) and food consumption (FC) compared to controls during the dosing
period).
There was a 7% decrease in gravid uterine weights, 6.5-fold increase in post-implantation
loss, 15% decrease in mean litter size, and 4.4-fold increase in the percentage of fetuses
dead or resorbed in litters with one or more live fetuses in HD females compared to
controls.
Skeletal variations of slight-delayed ossification were observed, which often are related
to maternal toxicity.
NOAEL for both maternal toxicity and embryo-fetal development is defined at
7 mg/kg/day based on decreased BWG and uterine weight of dams, and increased postimplantation loss and fetuses death/resorption associated with maternal toxicity.

Necropsy
findings
Offspring

All daprodustat-treated groups displayed increases in the litter and fetal percentages of
skeletal variations when compared to historical and concurrent controls. These findings
are not considered adverse as they represent developmental delays due to maternal
toxicity and would reverse.
Other noteworthy findings not included in the labeling based on low incidence (1 fetus in 1
litter/dose level) were:
 One fetus with a cleft in the palate and snout in the HD group. A cervical rib was
present at the 7th cervical vertebrae in two fetuses, one each in the 7 and
60 mg/kg/day groups.
 One fetus had a misaligned arch in a sacral vertebra and misaligned ileum in the
HD group.

Title/ Study Number: GSK1278863A: Oral (Stomach Tube) Once Daily Embryo Fetal Development
Study in the Dutch Belted Rabbit/ CD2009/00390/00

Key Study Findings





Maternal toxicity was observed with mortality, lower body weight gain up to 42%, and
abortion at HD of 60 mg/kg/day.
There were no fetal malformations associated with feed restriction. Other malformations
including irregularly shaped anterior fontanelle and manubrium, fused sternal centra,
small eye and socket, small lung, incompletely ossified sternal centra, and body edema
were noted at 60 mg/kg/day outside the historical control data range.
NOAELs were 30 mg/kg/day for maternal toxicity and for embryo-fetal toxicity based on
maternal lower body weight gain and fetal malformations at 60 mg/kg.

NDA 216951
Jesduvroq (daprodustat)
Parameter
Body weights and
food consumption

Major Findings
Maternal toxicity
Reduced body weight gain was observed at 4, 30, and 60 mg/kg/day of 33%, 25%,
42%, respectively, compared to controls at the end of the dosing period GDs 7-20.
During the postdose period of GDs 20-29, reduced BWG was 33% lower with
statistical significance at ≥ 30 mg/kg/day suggesting reversibility at the HD and
progression at the MD.
Overall BWG combining dosing and postdosing period of GDs 7-29, were lower by
17%, 26%, and 30% in 4, 30, and 60 mg/kg/day groups, respectively, vs. control.
There were no test article related food consumption changes observed other than
the HD females with early mortality.
Figure 45. Body Weight in Pregnant Rabbits Treated With GSK1277863A
Between Gestation Days 7-20

Source: Applicant’s submission

Necropsy findings
Cesarean section
data

No daprodustat-related macroscopic findings in dams were observed.
Litter size and the mean number of live fetuses were decreased with dose
dependency and statistical significance in daprodustat-treated groups, but the
finding was within the historical control data range.
Preimplantation and postimplantation loss were increased in daprodustat-treated
groups without a clear dose response and statistical significance, and the incidence
was within the historical control data range. The upper limit of the individual %
postimplantation loss in control group was high at 28.6%.

Study Number/ Title: GSK1278863A: Oral Study to Determine Concentrations of GSK1278863 and its
Metabolites in Rabbit Plasma (GlaxoSmithKline Study No. G12109)/ 2012N140611/00

Key Study Findings




The systemic exposure to daprodustat and its metabolites (M2, M3, M4, M5, M6, or
M13) were detected in pregnant rabbit plasma after oral dosing of 60 mg/kg/day once
daily between Gestation Days 7 to 11, which reflects that the fetuses are exposed to
daprodustat and its major metabolites during the organogenesis period.
Daprodustat exposure was minimum 16-fold higher compared to metabolites exposure in
plasma.

13.2.4.3. Prenatal and Postnatal Development (PPND)
Title/ Study Number: GSK1278863A + metabolites [GSK2391220A (M2), GSK2506104A (M3) and
GSK2531401A (M13)]: Oral Gavage and Subcutaneous Injection Tolerability, Toxicokinetics and Pre- and
Postnatal Developmental Dose Range Study in Pregnant Rats/ 2016N305715/00

Pregnant Sprague Dawley rats were treated with daprodustat at 0 (1% methylcellulose) or
60 mg/kg/day with (Group 2, 3) or without (Group 1) metabolite cocktail (M2, M3, and M13)
SC injection twice daily (6 hours between doses). A metabolite cocktail was prepared at 2
different sets of doses, 1X or 3X: 2.5, 3.2, or 1.8 mg/kg/day or 7.5, 9.6, or 5.4 mg/kg/day of M2,
M3, or M13, for Group 2 and 3, respectively. Main study animals were dosed from GD 6 to LD
6 and TK study animals were dosed from GD 6 to GD 10. The extended duration study (Group
4-7) with a decreased dosing regimen of 0, 10 or 40 mg/kg/day daprodustat was conducted with
or without the metabolite cocktail (2.5, 3.2, or 1.8 mg/kg/day, M2, M3, and M13, respectively).

Key Study Findings
There were 2 mortalities including one in control group and one in 60 mg/kg/day group with 3X
metabolite cocktail (Group 3). The cause of death was not determined at the necropsy for the
control animal or the female in the 60 mg/kg/day + 3X metabolite group, however this female
lost 12 g of body weight from GD 20 to 21. Another 60 mg/kg/day + 3X metabolite
GSK12788763A-treated animal was prematurely terminated due to cannibalism of the F1 litter
(7/13).
Daprodustat-related effects were observed at 60 mg/kg/day with decreased mean maternal body
weight gain during late gestation of 44% or 60%, Group 2 and 3, respectively. Decreases of 30%
and 40% were also observed at 40 mg/kg/day without (Group 6) and with (Group 7) 1X
metabolite cocktail. Decreases in body weight compared to controls for 40 and 60 mg/kg/day
corresponded with decreased mean food consumption. Mean body weight gain/body weight loss
persisted at 60 mg/kg/day with decreased food consumption during the lactation period (53% or
90% compared to controls, Group 2 and 3, respectively) but the effects on body weight or food
consumption at 40 mg/kg/day resolved during LD 1 to 7.
In addition, there were increases in the duration of parturition of 16.8 and 20 min/pup, Group 2
and 3, respectively, at 60 mg/kg/day compared to controls mean of 6 minutes/pup. The increase
of stillborn pups and found dead/missing (presumed cannibalized) pups decreased mean pup
survival and litter sizes by LD 7 (22% decrease compared to control) at 60 mg/kg/day, and
decreased pup body weight up to ~30% from birth to PND 7 were also observed at
60 mg/kg/day. The findings at 40 mg/kg/day were similar to that of 60 mg/kg/day with slightly
less severity and trend of reversibility.
The NOAEL was defined at 10 mg/kg/day with Cmax 72.4 µg/mL and AUC0-t µg.h/mL, based on
maternal toxicity and reduced embryo-fetal survival at the next highest dose of 40 mg/kg/day,
suggesting metabolite levels may impact maternal toxicity and associated embryo-fetal toxicity.

NDA 216951
Jesduvroq (daprodustat)

Title/ Study Number: GSK1278863A + Metabolites [GSK2391220A (M2), GSK2506104A (M3) and
GSK2531401A (M13)]: Oral Gavage and Subcutaneous Injection Pre- and Postnatal Development Study in Rats/
2018N365151/00

Key Study Findings










There was one mortality in the F0 generation at the HD of 40 mg/kg/day that appears
daprodustat-related, 2 mortalities of unclear relationship, and 1 mortality that were
considered unrelated.
Body weight gain during LD 1 to 4 was lower in HD with corresponding lower food
consumption during GD 18-21 and LD 4-8 compared to controls.
The daprodustat-related lower survival of F1 generation was statistically significant and
noted based on the lower viability index on PND 1 to 4, smaller live litter size on PND 4
and 21, and increased number of dead pups on PND 2 to 4.
Slightly longer delivery time was noted in HD (3/24) compared to controls.
The systemic exposure to daprodustat in F0 generation rats increased as the dose
increased in a less than dose proportional manner and metabolites (M2, M3, or M13)
were detected in plasma at similar levels across the doses between GD 9 and LD 21.
There was daprodustat detected in F1 generation suckling pups, which suggests the
secretion of daprodustat into milk.
The maternal body weight gain of F1 generation was lower in HD (40 mg/kg/day)
females during gestation and lactation compared to controls.
Effects (e.g., reduced litter size) at the HD on the F2 generation fell within the historical
control range and were deemed not daprodustat-related.
NOAEL is determined at 7 mg/kg/day, based on maternal toxicity and reduced embryofetal survival at the next highest dose of 40 mg/kg/day.

F1 Generation (Postweaning)
Mortality
One LD (0.8 mg/kg/day) male was found dead on PND 69.
Body weights
The body weight gains of HD rats during PND 22-29 were lower in F1 generation males
(9%) and females (8%) with statistical significance compared to controls.

Clinical signs

Sexual
maturation

Maternal mean body weight gain of F1 generation was lower at HD (40 mg/mg/kg)
during gestation (GD 15-21) and 75% lower body weight gain on Lactation Days 1-7
was noted compared to controls with statistical significance.
Daprodustat-related dehydration and whole-body cold to touch were observed (18 pups
in 3 litters and 15 pups in 2 litters, respectively) in the preweaning period.
HD (40 mg/kg/day) rats presented localized swelling along the surface of the abdomen
starting from PND 53 (females) and PND 71 (males), and this finding was not reversed
until termination.
No drug-related findings observed.
Noteworthy findings include vaginal patency delayed by 0.9 day (statistically significant)
and lower mean number of implantations by 9% at HD of 40 mg/kg/day compared to
controls. These changes were within the historical control data range.
In males, there was no daprodustat-related effect on spermatogenesis in testes based
on the qualitative evaluation of spermatogenesis with normal progression of
spermatogenic cycle.

13.2.5. Juvenile Toxicology
Title/ Study Number: GSK1278863A: Oral Juvenile Toxicity Study in Rats/
2020N436444/00
Key Study Findings





At the MD and HD, there were daprodustat-related increases in the mean age of vaginal
patency considered secondary to decreased mean body weight/body weight gain. At the
HD, there was also increased estrous cycle length in females, considered secondary to
the effects on growth and the early evaluation of estrous cycles (PND 57 vs. 63 to 70).
Statistically significant increased RBC mass was observed at the HD.
Minimal decrease in femur length was noted at HD at the end of dosing period but the
decrease did not resolve in males and worsened in females at the end of recovery
period. This effect corresponded with decreased body weight gain at this dose, where
females had decreased mean body weight compared to controls through PND 43.

Major Findings
Mortality
There were 13 deaths observed (found dead, euthanized due to moribundity, or
presumed to be cannibalized), but only one death at MD that appeared to be drugrelated (dehydration and body weight loss with no evidence of trauma or gavage error).
There were no dose dependency and mortalities were even observed in controls,
therefore no mortalities could be clearly attributed to daprodustat in this study.
Clinical signs
Dose-related dehydration at MD (1/30) and HD (4/30) during dosing period was
observed.

weights/Body
PND 4 and 32, and the body weights were comparable between the control and drug

treatment groups by the end of dosing period. Changes in BWG was not correlated
with decreased food consumption.

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 216951
Jesduvroq (daprodustat)
Parameter
Microscopic
findings

Major Findings
Increased cellularity in the bone marrow of sternum and incidence and/or severity of
extramedullary hematopoiesis in the spleen were observed at HD at the end of dosing
period (PND 64). The Applicant explains that the increased cells in the bone marrow
and spleen were predominately of the erythroid lineage. This finding is likely from the
pharmacological effect of drug increasing hematopoiesis.
Dilatation of the kidney was noted at low incidence in daprodustat-treated males at ≥
2/0.8 mg/kg/day.
Increased hepatocellular vacuolation was observed in HD females.

Toxicokinetic

Minimal focal epidermal hyperplasia of the skin was noted in one HD female.
Daprodustat was detected in plasma. The systemic exposure (AUC0-24hr and Cmax)
increased with dose increment and the increase was less than dose-proportional. The
daprodustat levels were maintained through PND 63 and peaked at PND 21 despite
lowering the doses, although dosing was more frequent.
There was no evidence of gender difference (< 2-fold) in systemic exposure to
daprodustat. The Tmax ranged between 1.5 – 12 hours, with increased Tmax on PND 12
(dose dependent).

13.2.6.1. Summary of Genetic Toxicology Studies
Genotoxicity Studies With Daprodustat
Study No./ Study Title: 2012N139517/00/ GSK1278863A: Oral Liver Comet Assay in
Crl:CD(SD) Rats
Key Study Findings
Male Sprague Dawley rats (n=5/group) were treated with 2 oral doses of 250, 500 or
1000 mg/kg/day daprodustat, approximately 21 to 22 hours apart. The vehicle used was 1% w/v
aqueous methylcellulose.
Daprodustat was not genotoxic in vivo Comet assay in the rat liver.

13.2.7. In Vitro
Bacterial Mutation Assays
M2, M3, M4, M5, M6, or M13 were evaluated at concentrations up to 5000 µg/plate in the
absence or presence of an in vitro metabolic activation system (S9-mix) in bacterial mutation

13.2.8. In Vivo
Title / Study Number: 2011N124885/00/ GSK2531401A: Intravenous Liver Comet Assay in
Crl:CD1(Icr) Mice
Key Study Findings
Male Crl:CD1 (ICR) mice (n=5-6/group) were treated with 2 intravenous (IV) doses of 0 (0.9%
(w/v) aqueous sodium chloride), 25, 100, 200, or 300 mg/kg GSK2531401A (Metabolite 13;
M13), approximately 21 hours apart with a single harvest 3 hours after the last dose. One oral
gavage dose of 30 mg/kg Ethyl Methane-Sulfonate (EMS) was used as positive control. 4
independent studies were conducted; however, due to a technical failure of the electrophoresis
procedure, the Comet data for one study were invalidated and have not been reported.
Mice given 300 mg/kg M13 convulsed and died immediately following dosing. A mild increase
in alanine aminotransferase activity was observed in 3/6 mice given 200 mg/kg/day. In these
mice, a corresponding minimal increase in aspartate aminotransferase activity was also noted.
Highest individual values in this group (no. 102) were 265 and 263 U/L for alanine
aminotransferase and aspartate aminotransferase, respectively. M13 was negative at 25 or
100 mg/kg/day across 3 independent studies. Increase of % Tail DNA was evident at the
maximum tolerated dose (MTD) 200 mg/kg/day GSK2531401A (M13). But the positive result
was not reproducible across 3 independent studies. Therefore, the result of Comet assay is
equivocal.

Carcinogenicity Study
Title/ Study Number: GSK1278863A: A 2-Year Oral (gavage) Carcinogenicity Study in Rats/
2014n196080

Key Study Findings
A daprodustat-related neoplastic finding was observed. The incidence of fibroadenoma in the
mammary gland at HD (4 mg/kg/day) in males was statistically significant and the incidence rate
was approximately 13% which exceeds the historical control data range (maximum incidence
rate 6.52%). However, the incidence of the combination of adenoma and fibroadenoma in the
mammary gland was not statistically significant. Regardless of the daprodustat-related
fibroadenoma findings in males, the clinical relevance of fibroadenoma in the mammary gland of
males is considered to be low.
